The retrospective data collection of additional data from patients with HST-TMA occurred after
all 28 patients were enrolled. Ideally, response criteria should be pre-defined.
6.2.1.1. Key Inclusion and Exclusion Criteria for HSCT-TMA
Patients
Inclusion Criteria
•

Age ≥18 years at screening

•

Patients with persistent HSCT-TMA were defined as having all of the following at least 2
weeks following modification or discontinuation of calcineurin inhibitor treatment or at
least 30 days after the transplant:
— Platelet count <150,000/µL
— Evidence of microangiopathic hemolysis (presence of schistocytes, serum LDH >
upper limit of normal (ULN), or haptoglobin < LLN)
— Renal dysfunction (doubling of serum creatinine from pretransplant)

•

No clinically apparent alternative explanation for thrombocytopenia and anemia.

•

If sexually active and of childbearing potential, must have agreed to practice a highly
effective method of birth control until the end of the study

Exclusion Criteria
•

Had eculizumab therapy within 3 months prior to screening

•

Had Shiga toxin-producing Escherichia coli-induced HUS

•

Had a positive direct Coombs test

•

Had an active systemic bacterial or fungal infection requiring antimicrobial therapy
(prophylactic antimicrobial therapy administered as standard of care was allowed)
32

Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
•

Had a poor prognosis with a life expectancy of less than 3 months in the opinion of the
Investigator

•

Were pregnant or lactating

•

Had received treatment with an investigational drug or device within 4 weeks prior to
screening

•

Had abnormal liver function tests defined as alanine aminotransferase (ALT) or aspartate
aminotransferase (AST) >5 times ULN

Clinical reviewer comment
The Applicant also submitted patient narratives to better characterize the HSCT-TMA
population. The inclusion and exclusion criteria define a HSCT-TMA patient population with
evaluable endpoints.
6.2.1.2. Study Endpoints
Primary Endpoint
The primary efficacy endpoint was the proportion of HSCT-TMA patients who responded to
narsoplimab-wuug treatment. Treatment response was defined as a patient with HSCT-TMA
who demonstrated both an improvement in laboratory TMA markers (Requirement 1; platelet
count and LDH) as well as improvement in clinical status (Requirement 2; either improvement in
organ function or freedom from transfusion) at any time point post baseline during the study.
Requirement 1: Improvement in laboratory TMA markers
•

Improvement in platelet count
— For patients with baseline platelet count ≤20,000/μL: tripling of baseline platelet
count AND postbaseline platelet count >30,000/μL AND no platelet transfusions 2
days before and on the day of the platelet count collection
— For patients with baseline platelet count >20,000/μL: increase in platelet count ≥50%
AND postbaseline platelet count >75,000/μL AND no platelet transfusions 2 days
before and on the day of the platelet count collection

•

Improvement in LDH is defined as LDH <1.5 times ULN

Requirement 2: Improvement in clinical status OR freedom from transfusion
•

Improvement in organ function
— Improvement in renal function as evidenced by any of the following: 1) a reduction of
creatinine >40%; 2) creatinine < ULN and reduction of creatinine >20%; or 3)
discontinuation of renal replacement therapy (RRT)
— Improvement in pulmonary function as evidenced by either of the following criteria:
1) extubation and discontinuation of ventilator support; or 2) discontinuation of
noninvasive mechanical ventilation (continuous positive pressure ventilation).
33

Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
— Improvement in neurological function as evidenced by either of the following criteria:
1) improvement in reversible neurological conditions (e.g., cessation of seizures); or
2) stabilization of irreversible neurological conditions (e.g., stability of neurological
deficits following stroke without further deterioration or subsequent strokes). The
neurological deficits considered for improvement were limited to stroke, seizures,
posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome (PRES), and weakness.
— Improvement in gastrointestinal function (gastrointestinal HSCT-TMA requires
diagnosis by tissue biopsy) as measured by improvement in the gastrointestinal
staging in the Mount Sinai Acute GVHD International Consortium (MAGIC) criteria
•

Freedom from transfusion defined as no transfusions for at least 4 weeks from the last
transfusion

Secondary Endpoints
•

100-day survival, from the date of TMA diagnosis

•

Overall survival, from the date of TMA diagnosis

•

Freedom from platelet transfusion defined as no platelet transfusions for at least 4 weeks
following the last platelet transfusion except for patients who died within 4 weeks of the
last platelet transfusion (only evaluated in patients who had platelet transfusions in the 2
weeks prior to or on the first narsoplimab-wuug dose date)

•

Freedom from RBC transfusion defined as no RBC transfusions for at least 4 weeks
following the last RBC transfusion except for patients who died within 4 weeks of the last
RBC transfusion (only evaluated in patients who had RBC transfusions in the

•

2 weeks prior to or on the first narsoplimab-wuug dose date)

•

Change from baseline in platelet count (postbaseline platelet counts without platelet
transfusions 2 days before and on the day of the platelet count collection were used) over
time using central laboratory values

•

Change from baseline in LDH over time using central laboratory values

•

Change from baseline in haptoglobin over time using central laboratory values

•

Change from baseline in hemoglobin (postbaseline hemoglobin without RBC and whole
blood transfusions 6 days before and on the day of the hemoglobin collection were used)
over time using central laboratory values

•

Change from baseline in creatinine over time in patients who had no reason other than
HSCT-TMA for creatinine elevation using central laboratory values

•

Duration of response, defined as the number of days from the first response date to the
first relapse date

Clinical reviewer comment
A Type A meeting with the Applicant occurred on May 2, 2019, in which endpoints were
discussed. The Agency and Applicant reached agreement on response based on LDH and
34
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
transfusion freedom. The Agency pointed out concerns regarding interpretation of magnitude of
platelet improvement and renal improvement. These components were later agreed to after
correspondence with the Applicant in written communication on July 9, 2019. The Agency
commented that there may be some overlap (i.e., presence of both TMA and GVHD) and that the
Applicant will need to address this in narratives and discussions for each patient. In a Type A
meeting on January 6, 2020 the Agency stated the evaluation of weakness is difficult in patients
with multiple comorbidities and who require intensive care treatment. There may be multiple
causes for a patient’s weakness therefore the Agency did not agree with the assessment of
weakness to assess neurological improvement. In addition, The Agency stated improvement in
confusion, agitation, headache will be difficult to assess given multitude of contributing factors
to these signs and symptoms.
Statistical Analysis Plan
Sample Size Determination
The sample size for each cohort (aHUS, and HSCT TMA/TTP) was determined by comparison
to the total sample size of uncontrolled Phase 2 studies that provided sufficient data for approval
of eculizumab for the treatment of aHUS. The Applicant stated that 28 patients are adequate
because of the orphan disease status. The Applicant stated that it was expected that
approximately 28 HSCT-TMA patients enrolled with observed response rate of 50%, results in
the exact 95% CI 30.6%, 69.4%.
Reviewer Comment
There was no pre-agreement between the Agency and the Applicant that 28 patients was an
adequate sample size.
Control
Study TMA-001 is a single arm study, neither concurrent control nor historical control was
utilized.
Reviewer comment
During the development of narsoplimab-wuug the possibility of establishing a historical control
was discussed, and the Agency pointed out potential barriers with this approach. A single-arm
trial based on response endpoint could possibly provide sufficient magnitude of treatment effect
and be acceptable to support the biologics license application (BLA) submission given the rarity
of HSCT-TMA and the high risk for poor outcomes in a population with no approved therapies.
The Agency also agreed with the modification of the primary endpoint in Amendment 11.1 in
order to assess response in patients with HSCT-TMA and the additional data collection.
Randomization
This was a single arm trial with no randomization required.

35
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
Interim Analysis
No interim analysis was planned.
Missing Data
Non-responder imputation was used to handle missing data for the binary efficacy endpoints.
Time-weighted average analysis and repeated measure model was used to analyze the continuous
secondary efficacy endpoints over time.
The TMA diagnosis date must have had at least the month and the year. If the day was missing,
the day was be imputed as 1.
For patients whose death dates were incomplete, if only the day of the death date was missing,
the day was imputed as the first day of the month. If either the month or the year of the death
date was missing, the death date was not imputed, and the overall survival was censored at the
last known date of alive in the database. For patients who were alive, overall survival was
censored at the last known date of alive in the database.
Primary Efficacy Analysis
Efficacy analyses were performed for the full analysis set (FAS) population and the Per-protocol
Patient Set (PPS) population. The FAS population included all HSCT-TMA patients who
received any amount of study drug. The PPS population included all HSCT-TMA patients who
received at least 4 weeks of study treatment (treatment period was greater than or equal to 28
days).
Analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint used a non-responder imputation to impute missing or
incomplete data. The number and percent of responders were summarized with an exact 95% CI
(Clopper-Pearson) by dose and total for the FAS and PPS populations. If the lower bound of the
exact 95% CI was greater than 15%, the sponsor determined that there was sufficient evidence to
demonstrate efficacy of narsoplimab-wuug in the target patient population.
Statistical reviewer comment
In the original BLA submission, the Applicant did not provide an adequate justification for the
chosen 15% response rate in patients with HSCT-TMA. The Agency recommended that the
Applicant conduct a literature search to justify 15% response rate (see Section 6.2.2 below).
Subgroup Analysis
•

Subgroup analyses for the following subgroups was performed for the FAS population:
— Age (<65, ≥65 years)
— Gender (Male or Female)
— GVHD (Yes or No)
— Significant infection (Yes or No)
36

Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
— Graft failure or non-engraftment (Yes or No)
— Female-to-male transplant (Yes or No)
— Multiple organ TMA involvement (Yes or No)
— Mismatched donor (Yes or No)
— Baseline platelet counts (<20,000 or ≥20,000 platelets/µL)
— Renal dysfunction at baseline (Creatinine > ULN or spot urine protein ≥2+ or
>30 mg/dL; Yes or No)
— Any transfusions within the 2 weeks prior to or on the first OMS721 dose date (Yes
or No)
•

A completer analysis was performed using the patients with non-missing responses only.
The exact 95% CI for the completer response rate was provided for the FAS population by
dose.

•

Two alternative definitions of response were analyzed:
— The first alternative response was defined as achieving an improvement in TMA
markers and an improvement in renal function. The number and percent of the
patients achieving the first alternative response was summarized with an exact 95%
CI by dose for the FAS population.
— The second alternative response was defined as the same as the primary endpoint by
excluding the neurological function. The number and percent of the patients
achieving the second alternative response were summarized with an exact 95% CI by
dose for the FAS population.

Secondary Efficacy Analyses
The following secondary efficacy endpoints were proposed:
•

100-day Survival: The 100-day survival status was determined as a binary variable. A
patient achieved a 100-day survival if the overall survival from the date of HSCT-TMA
diagnosis of the patient would have been greater than or equal to 100 days. If the 100-day
survival status of a patient was unknown (i.e., censored before 100 days), this patient
would have been considered to have died prior to 100 days. The 100-day survival rate was
estimated with an exact 95% CI by dose for the FAS population.

•

Overall Survival: Overall survival was calculated from the date of HSCT-TMA diagnosis.
For patients who were alive, their overall survival was censored at the last known date of
alive in the database. Overall survival was analyzed using Kaplan Meier method by dose
for the FAS population. Median overall survival and its 95% CI was estimated by dose.

•

Duration of Response: Duration of response was defined as the number of days from the
first response date to the first relapse date (first relapse date – first response date +1).
Duration of response was censored at the last platelet or LDH collection date if relapse did
not occur during the study. Kaplan-Meier analysis of the duration of response was

Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
performed for the patients who achieved a response by dose for the FAS population.
Median duration of response was presented with 95% CI.

Efficacy Results-Primary Endpoint
In the FAS, the Applicant’s results showed that 17 of 28 patients with HSCT-TMA (61%)
responded to narsoplimab-wuug treatment (Table 10), and the 95% CI for response was 40.6% to
78.5%. This response rate was statistically superior to the protocol-specified response rate in this
high-risk population of 15% (p<0.0001). As a reminder the PPS population include all HSCTTMA patients who received at least 4 weeks of study treatment (treatment period is greater than
or equal to 28 days). The Applicant’s results based on PPS appear to support their findings based
on FAS population.
Within this overall composite response endpoint, the Applicant’s results suggest that each of the
3 contributing clinical measures showed improvement in the FAS: 17 of 28 patients (61%)
showed improvement in both platelet count and LDH. However, FDA’s evaluation showed that
12 of 28 patients (43%) had improvement in both platelet count and LDH; 20 of 27 patients
(74%) had improvement in at least one organ function; 12 of 25 patients (48%) experienced
freedom from RBC and/or platelet transfusion after narsoplimab-wuug treatment (see Table 10).
The clinical team could not conclude a treatment effect for the overall composite endpoint nor
for the individual parameters below, see Section 6.3.1 for further details.

A detailed summary of the Applicant’s results for improvement in platelet count and LDH in
patients treated with narsoplimab-wuug is provided in Table 11 for both FAS and PPS. In
particular, improvement in platelet count was assessed in patients relative to their baseline
platelet count. The Applicant’s results demonstrated that in patients with very low baseline
platelet counts (20,000 or lower), 3 of 6 patients (50%) showed improvement (defined as a
platelet count at any time point that was greater than 30,000 and at least 3-fold above baseline
platelet levels); In patients with baseline platelet counts over 20,000, 11 out of 17 patients (65%)
showed improvement (defined as a platelet count at any time point that was greater than 75,000
and 1.5-fold or more above baseline platelet levels). Twenty-one (75%) of the patients showed
improvement in LDH (defined as an LDH <1.5 times the ULN). There was an overall
improvement in TMA markers in 17 patients (61%).

Reviewer Comment
The protocol stated that if a patient is not evaluable for a response criterion due to confounding
factors, the non-evaluable response criterion will not be included in the determination of
responder. Evaluability of each response criterion will be determined by a clinical review of
relevant data. The review team did not agree with this allowance which is further discussed in
Section 6.3.1.
A detailed summary of the Applicant’s results for improvement in organ function in all patients
(FAS) with HSCT- TMA and in the PPS is provided in Table 12. As seen from the table, the
Applicant reported that twenty of 27 patients (74%) experienced improvement in at least 1 organ
function in the FAS with the majority evaluated on the basis of renal function (27/28 patients,
96.4%). Eighteen of the 27 patients (67%) showed improvement in renal function, with 10
patients (37%) showing a reduction of creatinine levels of greater than 40%, and 15 patients
(56%) recorded creatinine levels less than the ULN and reduction of creatinine >20%. Of the 6
patients with neurologic dysfunction at baseline, 3 patients (50%) showed improvement in
evaluable neurologic function. These improvements were all in reversible neurological
conditions, and there was no stabilization of irreversible conditions. Improvements in organ
function in the PPS were generally similar to those in the FAS. with 17 of 22 patients (77%)
experiencing an improvement in organ function.

The Applicant’s results showed that there were 25 patients in the FAS who had required an RBC
or platelet transfusion in the 2 weeks leading up to study entry. Of these patients, 12 (48%)
experienced freedom from transfusion for a consecutive 4-week period after narsoplimab-wuug
treatment. The patients showing freedom from transfusion were made up of 8 of 18 patients
(44%) who had required platelet transfusion and 11 of 22 (50%) patients who had required RBC
transfusion prior to narsoplimab-wuug treatment. Results in the PPS were generally similar to
those in the FAS, with 11 of 20 patients (55%) experiencing freedom from transfusion.

Sensitivity Analyses
No response status was missing. Therefore, the analysis with non-missing responses was
identical to the primary analysis. For TMA markers and renal function only, the FDA’s analysis
results showed that 12 of 28 (43% with 95% CI, 24.5% to 62.8%) patients in the FAS responded
to treatment.
Efficacy Results-Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
100-Day and Overall Survival
A summary of 100-day survival and overall survival (OS) following narsoplimab-wuug
treatment in the FAS, PPS, and treatment responders from the FAS is shown in Table 14.
Survival days were calculated from the day of TMA diagnosis. At Day 100 after HSCT-TMA
diagnosis, 19 patients (68%) remained alive in the FAS, and the median OS was 274 days. For
the PPS, the 100-day survival rate was 83%, and the median OS was 361 days. Among patients
who responded to narsoplimab-wuug treatment, 100-day survival rate was 94% while OS was
not estimable.

Clinical reviewer comment
The definition of 100-day survival was defined as survival 100 days after diagnosis of HSCTTMA and the definition of overall survival was survival after diagnosis of HSCT-TMA. In the
transplant setting there are different definitions of survival as well as different definitions used in
the literature. In the transplantation setting, 100-day survival is often defined as survival 100
days after the transplantation date (Day 0) and overall survival is defined as survival after date
of transplantation. Therefore, it is difficult to interpret any survival data due to differences in
definitions and lack of adequate control.

Table 15 provides a summary of durability (no relapse) and duration of response for the FAS
population based on the FDA analyses. Out of 12 responders, 9 were without relapse. Median
duration of response was 617 days (95% CI: 185,822 days).

6.2.2. Historical Literature Review
6.2.2.1. Design, Literature Historical Review
During the review of the application, FDA requested additional data to justify the 15% response
rate in untreated patients to further evaluate the clinical benefit of narsoplimab-wuug in patients
with HSCT-TMA to serve as a control group for OMS-TMA-00. The Applicant submitted the
literature based historical control on April 23, 2021. Because of Due the extent of the additional
review required, a major amendment was issued on May 19, 2021, extending the user fee goal
date by 3 months.
Objectives: Describe the natural history of HSCT-TMA in adult patients following allogeneic
transplant.
•

Describe the demographic and baseline characteristics of adult patients with HSCT-TMA
reported in the literature

•

Describe the literature-reported frequency of HSCT-TMA improvement/resolution in the
following adult patient populations:
— All reported patients except those noted to have received treatment with eculizumab
and/or defibrotide (AP group)
— All reported patients in the AP group except those noted to have received any
pharmacological treatment (nonpharmacologically treated patients, or NPTP). These
excluded therapies: rituximab, vincristine, pravastatin, recombinant thrombomodulin,
etanercept, prednisone, mycophenolate mofetil, cyclosporin, imatinib, doxycycline,
limaprost. (NPTP group)

Reviewer comment
The withdrawal or change in calcineurin inhibitors as initial management was not characterized
or described. This is a limitation of the data because CNI discontinuation is a common step in
TMA management.
50
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
•

Describe the literature-reported 100- day survival following HSCT-TMA diagnosis in the
following adult patient populations:
— All reported patients except those noted to have received treatment with eculizumab
and/or defibrotide group (AP group)
— All reported patients except those noted to have received any pharmacological
treatment (NPTP group as above)

•

Describe the impact of the following risk factors on the improvements/resolution of
HSCT-TMA and 100- day survival following HSCT-TMA diagnosis:
— Co-existing GVHD
— Co-existing infection
— Co-existing neurological findings
— Co-existing pulmonary complications
— Co-existing renal dysfunction
— Cp-existing gastrointestinal complications
— Relapsed or persistent underlying malignancy

•

Compare the response and 100-day survival rates in the literature- based patient groups to
the response and 100-day survival rates observed with narsoplimab-wuug in Study TMA001.
6.2.2.2. Eligibility Criteria, Literature Historical Control

The study included patients from the published literature (dated year 20000 and later) only if the
met all of the following criteria:
•

Age >18 reported in the article

•

Diagnosis of HSCT-TMA following allogenic HSCT

•

Identifiable patient-level data

•

Data including (i) any variable of the primary response variable (platelet count, lactate
dehydrogenase, organ function or transfusions) used in Study TMA-001 or (ii) report of
response and at least 1 risk factor present in the narsoplimab-wuug pivotal trial patients
(i.e., GVHD, neurological dysfunction, infection, pulmonary dysfunction, renal
dysfunction, gastrointestinal dysfunction, relapse, or persistence of underlying
malignancy)

Exclusion
•

Patients from the published literature were excluded from the study if they had received
eculizumab and/or defibrotide therapy.

Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
Data Sources
•

The study procedures were limited to review of the published English-language literature
generated through a PubMed search.

Methods
The following was sued to conduct the literature review:
•

Criteria for publication selection were:
— Reports of adult patients receiving allogeneic transplants
— Reports with patient level data including (i) any component of the primary response
endpoint used in the narsoplimab-wuug pivotal trial or (ii) report of response and at
least 1 risk factor present in the narsoplimab-wuug pivotal trial patients
— Publication after 2000

•

A PubMed search was conducted of the following terms:
— “Thrombotic microangiopathy” and “stem cell
— “Transplant-associated thrombotic microangiopathy”
— “Cancer associated thrombotic microangiopathy

•

All unique, English-language articles that met the criteria above were collected.
Primary review of each full publication not abstracts was conducted by at least 2
independent reviewers with medical backgrounds. All articles identified by either
reviewer were forward for secondary review by different individuals.
Working independently, 2 physicians performed a secondary review of publications
identified in the primary review to confirm correct inclusion of publications in the
literature review. Inclusion of publications was based on meeting the criteria for
publication selection. A third physician adjudicated any differences.
All relevant data were extracted by one of the secondary review physicians compiled. A
medical writer conducted a quality control review on all extracted data against all
publications passing the secondary review.
All articles identified in the primary review but rejected in the review were provided.
secondary
Operational definitions of the response components from Study TMA-001 and criteria for
improvement in the literature patients are:

Platelet Count
Evidence of platelet count improvement was considered a positive platelet count response in the
literature patients. Platelet count improvement due to platelet transfusions were not considered a
platelet count response in the literature patients.

52
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
LDH
Evidence of lactate dehydrogenase improvement was considered a positive LDH response in
literature patients
Renal Function
Evidence of creatinine improvement or cessation of RRT was considered a positive renal
function response in the literate patients.
Neurological Function
Improvement in gastrointestinal function was defined as any improvement in gastrointestinal
function or bleeding reported in the literature patients.
Transfusion Burden
Transfusion freedom was defined as a report in the literature of transfusion freedom in a patient
previously receiving transfusions.
Risk Factor Definition
Any of the risk factors of graft-versus-host disease (GHVD), infection, relapse or persistence of
underlying malignancy and organ dysfunctions were defined as a report of such complication in
the article.
Response
Response were was defined as evidence in the article of reported “response” OR improvement in
platelet count, LDH, and either organ function or transfusion burden.
100-Day Survival
A patient achieved a 100-day survival if the overall survival from the date of HSCT_TMA
diagnosis of the patient was ≥100 days. If the 100-day survival status of a patient was unknown
(i.e., censored before 100 days), this patient was considered to have died prior to 100 days.
6.2.2.3. Statistical Analysis Plan, Literature Historical Review
Sample Size Determination
Not planned
Interim Analyses
Not planned

53
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
Missing Data
Different imputations were used to impute missing baseline characteristics and risk factors
information for all literature-based patients to enable treatment comparisons between
narsoplimab-wuug–treated and literature-control patients. These imputation methods aimed to
represent the best- and worst-case scenarios that would cover the actual risk-factor status of the
literature-based patients.
The risk-factor status of all narsoplimab-wuug–treated patients was known.
The reported demographics and risk factors from the literature studies were not consistent across
studies. In order to perform the propensity score analyses, unknown (i.e., not reported) risk
factors for the literature patients were imputed by 4 methods:
•

All unknown risk factors are imputed as absent.

•

All unknown risk factors are imputed as present.

•

Unknown risk factors are imputed as present for responders, and absent for
nonresponders.

•

Unknown risk factors are imputed as absent for responders, and present for
nonresponders.

Exploratory Analyses
The Applicant conducted post-hoc exploratory analyses include propensity score analyses, where
propensity scores were estimated by a logistic regression for treatment with the following
covariates: age and days from HSCT to TMA diagnosis as continuous variables, and GVHD,
infection, renal dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction as categorical variables (Yes/No).
Pulmonary dysfunction, GI dysfunction and relapsed/refractory primary disease were not used
because of the large number of unknown (i.e., not reported) statuses in the literature database.
•

Model 1 included: Age, days from HSCT to TMA diagnosis, GVHD, infection, renal
dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction

•

Model 2 included: Age, GVHD, infection, renal dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction

•

Model 3 included: Days from HSCT to TMA diagnosis, GVHD, infection, renal
dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction

•

Model 4 included: GVHD, infection, renal dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction

Weighted logistic regression for response with a single treatment covariate (narsoplimab-wuug
or literature) weighted by stabilized inverse probability of treatment weighting (sIPTW) was
performed for each model and imputation method. Odds ratios with 95% CI were calculated. A
similar approach was taken for 100-day survival.
Sensitivity analyses using matching were performed on Model 1. 1:k matching (matching k
literature patients to 1 narsoplimab-wuug patient, where k =1 to 4) using a greedy matching
method with a caliper of 0.5 (with respect to the pooled standard deviation of the logit of the
54
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
propensity score from the 2 treatment groups) was performed for each imputation method. For
each matched set and imputation method, a conditional logistic regression for response with a
single treatment covariate (narsoplimab-wuug or literature) was performed. Odds ratios with
their 95% CIs were calculated.
Diagnostics were performed to evaluate goodness of fit for various models and imputations and
to evaluate balancing of risk factors in the matched patient cohorts.

Efficacy Results Literature Review
StudyTMA-001 utilized, endpoint is a composite endpoint which was comprised of an
improvement in LDH level and platelet count and a clinical improvement (organ function or
freedom from transfusion). There are no standard response criteria for HSCT-TMA and the,
collectively a clinically meaningful endpoint and a rigorous test for response to therapy. The
literature reports a variety of response criteria, which, for the most part, are not as rigorous as the
clinical trial endpoint. We have analyzed these criteria according to a definition of stringency as
follows:
Laboratory
Criteria or clinical criterion/criteria only = less stringent compared to Study TMA-001; LDH and
platelets and clinical criterion/criteria = “equal” stringency to the narsoplimab-wuug clinical trial
endpoint. The response criterion/criteria that were not defined in literature publications were
designated as incomplete.
Table 19 summarizes the breakdown for the 35 studies, using the definitions of stringency above,
that comprise the AP group and the corresponding response rates. While the NPTP group is most
comparable for the narsoplimab-wuug clinical trial population, the AP group, which includes
patients receiving therapeutic interventions, is included to provide the most conservative
analysis. As can be seen from Table 19, the literature-based response rates across stringency
levels range from approximately 29% to 37% in the AP group and 18% to 32% in the NPTP
group. For main Study TMA-001, the Applicant estimated response is 61% and FDA estimated
response is 43%.

6.3. Key Review Issues Relevant to Evaluation of
Benefit
6.3.1. Determination of Response Rate: Applicant’s vs.
FDA’s analysis
Issue
The primary endpoint of Study TMA-001 was the proportion of HSCT-TMA patients who
responded to narsoplimab-wuug treatment. There was no control group; therefore, accurate
interpretation of the response rate is critical in being able to demonstrate efficacy. Response rate
was determined based on improvement in platelets, a reduction in LDH, and clinical
improvement. The Applicant concluded that 61% [95% CI: 41%-78%] of patients met the
primary endpoint. There are challenges in interpreting the response in the setting of a very
complex patient population, disease related issues, endpoint issues and lack of adherence to the
protocol. The clinical team reviewed each patient narrative and provided adjudicated responses
for the 28 patients. The FDA concluded that the response rate was 43% [95% CI: 24%, 63%].
60
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
Background
Explanation Regarding Relevance of Review Issue
The patient population included in Study TMA-001 was complex, consisting of mostly critically
ill patients with multiple confounders (i.e., concurrent diseases, concomitant medications),
posing a challenge to determine the response rate.
The clinical review team assessed all 28 patient narratives submitted with the application. What
became apparent during the review is that while the primary endpoint had mostly objective
components (i.e., platelet count, LDH, serum creatinine, transfusion freedom, increase in
haptoglobin) the complexity of the patient population, comorbid illnesses, along with trial design
features, impacted the interpretability.
Specific issues identified that impacted the interpretation of the response to narsoplimab-wuug
can be further categorized by disease-related issues, lack of adherence to protocol defined
enrollment and response criteria and difficulty in determining magnitude and durability in
endpoint (response). These included:
Disease-Related Issues
•
•

Concurrent diseases: The majority of patients had a diagnosis of GVHD, significant
infection, or other concurrent disease process for which they were actively treated for
around the time of HSCT-TMA diagnosis and/or narsoplimab-wuug administration.
Concurrent medications: Patients were receiving concomitant medications that may
impact the response to narsoplimab-wuug, such as modification of calcineurin inhibitors
or sirolimus, in addition to other therapies or treatments (i.e., plasma therapy, rituximab,
and defibrotide).

Lack of Adherence to Protocol Defined Enrollment and Response Criteria
•

•

Not all patients who were classified by the Applicant as responders met all three
components (platelet, LDH and improvement in organ function (renal, pulmonary, or
gastrointestinal) of the primary endpoint definition. deviating from protocol defined
response.
A significant number of patients did not meet the predefined inclusion/exclusion criteria
but were still included in the study. Magnitude of serum creatinine change may be
difficult to interpret for patients who did not meet the inclusion criteria of doubling of
serum creatinine from pre-HSCT level.

Difficulty in Determining Magnitude of Laboratory Changes and Durability of Response
•
•
•

The significance of magnitude of change was difficult to assess in patients with LDH just
above the ULN.
Difficultly interpreting improvement in neurologic and/or GI dysfunction as these clinical
manifestations could be multifactorial and were treated concurrently
Durability of response and time to response
61

Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
Assessment
Disease-Related Issues
Confounding Concurrent Disease and Treatments
GVHD and infections are common complications of HSCT. The basis of the pathophysiology of
HSCT-TMA involves endothelial injury and microthrombi formation (Khosla et al. 2018). Firstline treatment of HSCT-TMA is typically to limit and when possible, completely remove the
condition which is causing endothelial injury, which includes treatment of infections and GVHD
(Khosla et al. 2018). This may be an effective treatment strategy in particular when HSCT-TMA
is mild-moderate (Rosenthal 2016). The clinical review team evaluated the rates of infections
and GVHD in Study TMA-001 in order to assess the possible impact of these disease processes
on the primary endpoint.
In total, 19/28 (67.9%) of patients had a diagnosis of GVHD (acute and/or chronic) around the
time of HSCT-TMA diagnosis and/or during narsoplimab-wuug treatment. This included 12/17
(70.6%) responders and 7/11 (63.6%) nonresponders with GVHD. Patients were treated with
corticosteroids along with other medications used to treat GVHD.
In addition, most patients had significant infections. This included 23/28 (82.1%) of patients with
a significant infection. Table 21 shows significant infections as defined by the Applicant that
occurred 2 weeks prior to the first dose of narsoplimab-wuug dose or during the treatment
period. Of the responders 15/17 (88.2%) had significant infections and 9/11 (81.8%)
nonresponders had significant infections. Patients were treated with antibiotics, antifungals,
and/or antivirals depending on the type of infection.
While the clinical review team acknowledges that these concurrent diseases may increase the
risk of poor outcomes, it can be challenging to determine if the treatment impacted the response
in the setting of single-arm trial. Notably, responders and nonresponders had similar rates of
GVHD and infections.

Concomitant Medications
CNI or Sirolimus
While there are no approved therapies for the treatment of HSCT-TMA, one of the first
management steps includes removing the potential offending agent, such as CNIs and/or
sirolimus. The Bone Marrow Transplant Clinical Trials Network Toxicity Committee consensus
63
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
summary recommended CNI cessation as the first line management (Ho et al. 2005; Epperla et
al. 2020). Not all patients respond to this method of HSCT-TMA management. Replacing CNIs
with other immunosuppressive agents has been demonstrated to be more successful than CNI
dose reductions (Elsallabi et al. 2016). Experts have recently disputed the effectiveness of CNI or
sirolimus dose reductions and discontinuations, however. Recent cohort studies have suggested
that immunosuppression withdrawal does not impact patient outcomes (Li et al. 2019; Epperla et
al. 2020). Therefore, this issue remains controversial.
In an information request (IR) response to the Agency, the Applicant reported CNI change in
7/17 (41.2%) of responders and 7/11 (63.6%) of nonresponders. The clinical reviewer conducted
a separate analysis by reviewing the 28 narratives for CNI change or discontinuations in both
responders and nonresponders. CNI or sirolimus dose reduction or discontinuation was
considered relevant if it occurred either within 2 weeks before starting narsoplimab-wuug or
during treatment. In total, 10/17 (58.9%) of responders had CNI or sirolimus changes, compared
to 3/11 (27.3%) of nonresponders (Table 22).
While more responders had CNI and/or sirolimus modifications or discontinuations compared to
nonresponders, the significance of this is uncertain given there is no control arm in this study.

Plasma Therapy
As specified in the protocol, patients were allowed to receive plasma exchange while on study.
According the Applicant’s CSR, a total 7 patients (25%) received plasma therapy while on study.
Per protocol four patients received supplemental doses of narsoplimab-wuug at 2 mg/kg IV
while receiving narsoplimab-wuug. Of the patient’s that received extra doses of narsoplimabwuug, 2 (50%) were responders while receiving plasma exchange. While plasma exchange may
be an effective treatment for TTP, this mode of therapy is thought to be less beneficial in HSCTTMA. Plasma exchange has variable response rates and an unclear impact on mortality (Khosla
et al. 2018).
Rituximab
According to the Applicant’s CSR, 5 patients received rituximab as a concomitant medication.
Patients were allowed to receive rituximab therapy during the Study TMA-001. In reviewing the
concomitant medication dataset (adcm.jmp), 6 patients were identified who received rituximab
within the month (30 days) before receiving narsoplimab-wuug and/or during narsoplimab-wuug
treatment. Three out of 6 (50%) of patients were responders (Table 23).
65
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
While no large clinical trials have been conducted with rituximab in patients with HSCT-TMA,
there has been some sparse data suggesting a role for rituximab as a therapy option for patients
with HSCT-TMA (Jodele et al. 2015). An equal number of responders and nonresponders
received rituximab, although the overall number of patients receiving rituximab is small, and
interpretation may be confounded by the fact that 3 patients received plasma therapy as well.
Plasma therapy in combination with rituximab may be effective therapy in some patients (insert
reference).

confounding factors, the nonevaluable response criterion will not be included in the
determination of responder.” However, we do not agree with this approach as it is unknown if
data missingness would have affected the efficacy analysis. Patients with missing data should be
classified as non-responders.

(b) (6)

The patient was diagnosed with HSCT-TMA on day 21 post-HSCT. The patient entered
the clinical study on day 35. At the time of study entry, the platelet count was 17×109/L
(normal 130-400×109/L), LDH was 792 U/L (normal 125-220 U/L), creatinine was
0.73 mg/dL (normal 0.72-1.18 mg/dL), haptoglobin <3 g/L (normal 14-268 g/L), and the
patient was receiving transfusions. The patient started narsoplimab-wuug on day 38 and
received 8 weekly doses. Per the Applicant, the patient met a response (based on TMA
markers only) 7 days after starting narsoplimab-wuug. This was based on a LDH
reduction to 278 U/L. Platelet response was not evaluated as the patient had not engrafted
platelets and remained transfusion dependent. The patient met a clinical improvement
response of serum creatinine 37 days after starting narsoplimab-wuug with creatinine
reduction to 0.57 mg/dL (baseline creatinine was 0.76 mg/dL). The patient did not meet a
response based on transfusions. While not a component of the response definition, the
haptoglobin did normalize on treatment. Of note, the patient had a HSCT-TMA relapse
62 days after starting narsoplimab-wuug, although this was complicated by neutropenic
sepsis. The patient died shortly after. The full narrative can be found in the Section 16.
With a delayed modest improvement in creatinine, it is difficult to say that this is a robust
response. In addition, haptoglobin was not part of the primary response criteria. In terms
of a protocol defined response, this patient is likely a nonresponder.

Patient

(b) (6)

The patient was a 32-year-old male with a history of high-risk ALL who underwent a
matched related donor transplant. Day 1 post-transplant, he developed mucositis
requiring parental nutrition and opioid management. On day 6, he developed bacteremia
(blood culture was positive for staphylococcal aureus), he was treated initially with
meropenem. The patient achieved neutrophil and platelet engraftment on day 18 and 22,
respectively. On day 88, he was diagnosed with oral GVHD. His cyclosporine was
discontinued on day 145. On day 159, he had elevated liver function tests, liver biopsy
demonstrated severe panlobular hepatitis with hepatocyte necrosis, concerning for
aGVHD. The patient was treated with prednisone. He also had CMV reactivation.
Around day 178, he was diagnosed with acute skin and liver GVHD, in addition to
chronic GVHD of the eye.
On day 194 he was diagnosed with HSCT-TMA, based on thrombocytopenia, elevated
LDH, elevated creatinine and anemia. The patient had been restarted on cyclosporin two
weeks prior but this was discontinued once HSCT-TMA was diagnosed. At this time, he
was also diagnosed with PRES. With blood pressure control the patient recovered. He
also had a low-grade fever. He received his first dose of narsoplimab-wuug on day 201
and received 8 total doses. Response occurred 153 days after starting narsoplimab-wuug
and was based on improvement in LDH, platelets, neurologic dysfunction and
transfusions per the Applicant. Following initiation of narsoplimab-wuug he had
recurrent episodes of delirium and confusion, this was thought to be secondary to
steroids, and/or self-discontinuation of lorazepam and haloperidol.

It is the reviewer’s opinion that it is difficult to determine if the patient’s neurologic

HSCT-TMA, it appears the patient improved with blood pressure control, the timing of
the improvement in PRES is unclear in the patient narrative and therefore the impact of
narsoplimab-wuug is unclear. Euphoria, delirium and confusion occurred after
narsoplimab-wuug was started so it is therefore confounded by a new treatment and was
managed with multiple other therapies including; hyperglycemia control, steroid taper,
lorazepam and haloperidol. It is the reviewer’s opinion that this patient should be
considered a non-responder.

In summary, it is in the reviewer’s opinion that 5 patients
(b) (6)
did not meet all three components of the response definition and should not be
called responders.

Significant number of patients did not meet the predefined inclusion/exclusion criteria but were
still included in the study. Magnitude of serum creatinine change may be difficult to interpret for
patients who did not have the inclusion criteria of doubling of serum creatinine from pre-HSCT
level.
The Applicant states 11/28 (39%) of patients did not meet inclusion/exclusion criteria but were
granted waivers. Waivers were mostly granted for the creatinine criteria not being met (n=6) and
for a positive Coombs test (n=3). The Applicant’s rationale for waivers included; a diagnosis of
HSCT-TMA could be made based on other criteria, renal dysfunction is a late finding in TMA
progression and patients with HSCT-TMA may have a positive Coomb’s test due to transfusions.
A list of all patients who received a waiver and rationale why the investigator chose to continue
to enroll those patients in the study is listed in Table 113.
Exclusion Criteria of Positive Coombs Test
A positive Coombs test was an exclusion criterion in Study TMA-001. Despite this exclusion
criteria 3 patients were included in Study TMA-001 who had a positive Coombs test. Patient’s
diagnosed with HSCT-TMA typically have a negative Coombs test in order to differentiate
HSCT-TMA from Coombs positive autoimmune hemolytic anemia (AIHA) (Khosla et al. 2018).
AIHA occurs in 4-6% of patients following allogeneic stem cell transplantation (Barcellini et al.
2019). AIHA can have overlapping features of HSCT-TMA, such as hemolytic anemia, elevated
LDH, reduced haptoglobin, increase in transfusion requirements, and/or thrombocytopenia when
associated with Evan’s syndrome. Therefore, to ensure an accurate diagnosis of HSCT-TMA,
patients with a Coombs positive test should not be included in this study. Although, the
Applicant reports that the while the local laboratory reported a weakly positive Coombs test, the
central laboratory report was negative for all three patients. Therefore, in this reviewer’s opinion
it is acceptable to include these patients in the analysis.
Inclusion Criteria of Doubling Serum Creatinine
A key inclusion criterion for Study TMA-001 was doubling of serum creatinine from the preHSCT level. In total, 6 of the 28 patients did not meet the renal inclusion criteria of doubling of

70
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
serum creatinine, yet the Applicant claimed these patients had other laboratory markers or
clinical findings to support a diagnosis of HSCT-TMA.
While not all patients with HSCT-TMA have renal dysfunction, the kidney is the most common
organ affected (Khosla et al. 2018) and it is included in the diagnostic criteria by the Blood and
Marrow Transplant Clinical Trials Network (Elsallabi et al. 2016). While serum creatinine is an
imperfect marker for renal injury, this was a reasonable choice for inclusion criteria (doubling of
serum creatinine from baseline) and was agreed upon by the Agency during the clinical
development of Study TMA-001. Further, this provided an objective measurement for assessing
clinical improvement. Therefore, ignoring this inclusion criterion is an important protocol
(b) (6)
violation. One exception is patient
who had biopsy proven TMA.
Five of the 6 patients who did not meet renal inclusion criteria were still evaluated for the renal
(b) (6)
response endpoint. The one patient
who was not evaluated for a renal response had a
normal creatinine at baseline. Of the 5 patients who were evaluated for renal response, 3 were
(b) (6)
(b) (6)
responders
per the Applicant, however by the FDA only
(b) (6)
were considered responders by the FDA.
(b) (6)

Patient
was the only patient who was dependent on the renal response as the clinical
component to meet the definition of response. While the patient had an elevated serum creatinine
of 1.25 mg/dL at the time of HSCT-TMA diagnosis it had down trended to 0.78 mg/dL prior to
narsoplimab-wuug administration. While the serum creatinine further decreased to 0.6 mg/dL on
narsoplimab-wuug therapy, this change is not clinically meaningful. It is in the clinical
(b) (6)
reviewer’s opinion that patient
should not be considered a responder. Additionally, this
patient had a nonevaluable platelet count (see discussion on patients not meeting response
definition). The clinical benefit of narsoplimab-wuug in this patient is therefore not evident.
In addition, the Applicant reports improvement in renal function occurred in 18/27 (74%) of
patients. It is in the clinical reviewer’s opinion that renal function should only be evaluated in
patients who had doubling of serum creatinine from the pre-HSCT level, to ensure a significant
and meaningful magnitude of change. Considering only patients who had a doubling of serum
creatinine, the proportion of patients who had a renal response was 15/22 (68.2%).
In conclusion, the number of patient waivers for inclusion criteria reflects the challenges in the
diagnosis of HSCT-TMA and the lack of uniform diagnostic criteria as there can be a variety of
factors in diagnosis that may lead investigator to conclude a patient has HSCT-TMA. The
rationale for the waivers may not be unreasonable as change in renal function can be a late
finding and DAT positivity may be impacted by transfusions; however, this aspect of the clinical
course of TMA should have been considered at the protocol development stage.
Difficulty in Determining Magnitude of Response
The clinical significance of magnitude of change in LDH was difficult to assess in patients with
LDH values just above the upper limit of normal.
Inclusion criteria included laboratory values consistent with microangiopathic hemolysis,
including LDH above the upper limit of normal; this is consistent with most diagnostic criteria
71
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
for HSCT-TMA (Elsallabi et al. 2016). Most patients included in Study TMA-001 had a
significantly elevated LDH value with a mean LDH of 580.5U/L (range 237-1140U/L), normal
range can vary depending upon laboratory but usually range between 140U/L to 280U/L,
consistent with hemolysis (although LDH can increase due a variety of factors i.e., tissue
(b) (6)
damage, liver injury, bone injuries, infections and more). There were 2 patients (
(b) (6)
) who were responders that met the inclusion criteria of LDH, but the LDH value was
just above the upper limit of normal. The LDH values of these patients are described below. As
the criteria for LDH response was based on an LDH <1.5x ULN, it is difficult to determine the
significance of LDH reduction as the patients described below already had an LDH <1.5x ULN
prior to narsoplimab-wuug treatment.

(normal range: 125-220 U/L). Baseline LDH was 237 U/L (normal range: 125-220). LDH at time

Although the patient had a decrease of LDH per the protocol defined criteria, the magnitude of
change is small.
Patient

(b) (6)

The patient was diagnosed with HSCT-TMA on day 76, LDH was not reported in the patient
narrative. Screening LDH was 290 U/L (normal range: 125-220 U/L). Baseline LDH was 261
U/L (normal range: 125-220 U/L). LDH at time of first meeting response definition was 187 U/L
(normal range: 125-220). Lowest LDH following narsoplimab-wuug treatment was 104 U/L
(normal range: 125-220). See patient narrative in Section 16.
Clinical reviewer comment
While the patient had a LDH just above the ULN at baseline, the clinical reviewer still believes it
is likely the patient had a diagnosis of HSCT-TMA. The patient had other features of HSCT-TMA
including thrombocytopenia, anemia, increased serum creatinine, and schistocytes. While the
interpretation of LDH response is limited with a baseline value just ULN, the patient had a
reduction in serum creatinine and improvement in platelet count. To further support response the
patient also had improvement in haptoglobin and hemoglobin.
(b) (6)

In conclusion, while both patients
) had mildly an elevated LDH, diagnosis
of HSCT-TMA and response could be confidently determined based on other criteria. The
clinical reviewer agrees both patients are responders.

Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
Neurologic Dysfunction
Improvement in neurological function was defined in the protocol as; (1) improvement in
reversible neurological conditions (e.g., cessation of seizures); or 2) stabilization of irreversible
neurological conditions (e.g., stability of neurological deficits following stroke without further
deterioration or subsequent strokes). The neurological deficits considered for improvement were
limited to stroke, seizures, PRES, and weakness. Improvement in neurologic function was
evaluable in 6 out of 28 (21.4%) of patients (Table 24). Of those 6 patients in which neurologic
function could be evaluated, 3 were responders, narratives describing neurologic improvement
are included below.
In a Type A meeting on January 6, 2020, the Applicant asked the Agency if they agreed with the
proposal to assess neurologic improvement. The Agency responded that they did not agree due to
the difficulty assessing improvement in the intensive care setting. In addition, the Agency had
the following comments: “The evaluation of weakness is difficult in patients with multiple
comorbidities and who require intensive care treatment. There may be multiple causes for a
patient’s weakness therefore we do not agree with the assessment of weakness to assess
neurological improvement. In addition, please note that improvement in confusion, agitation,
headache will be difficult to assess given multitude of contributing factors to these signs and
symptoms.” Therefore, weakness while included as a neurologic deficit for evaluation, was not
agreed upon by the Agency.
Neurologic improvement was difficult to assess in all three patients, this is described in more
detail in the narratives below. Neurologic manifestations included PRES, confusion, delirium,
and weakness, all of which can be multifactorial (i.e., concomitant medications, deconditioning,
and concurrent illnesses) and were treated during with other therapies along with narsoplimab(b) (6)
wuug. For example, one patient
, had a diagnosis of Guillain Barré syndrome (GBS) at
the time of HSCT-TMA diagnosis, therefore it is difficult to determine if the weakness
experienced by the patient was due to GBS or HSCT-TMA. Overall, the treatment effect on
neurologic manifestations of HSCT-TMA is difficult to interpret.
Narratives for Patients With Improvement in Neurologic Dysfunction
(b) (6)

Approximately two weeks prior to the diagnosis of HSCT-TMA the patient developed
polyneuropathy with profound weakness restricting the patient to bed. The patient experienced
improvement in polyneuropathy clinically and based on electrophysiological evaluation while
receiving narsoplimab-wuug. The neurologic response occurred approximately 27 days after
starting narsoplimab-wuug.
Neuropathy is not a common manifestation of HSCT-TMA. While temporally it appears that
narsoplimab-wuug may have improved the patient’s polyneuropathy, one must also consider that
neuropathy can be multifactorial. For example, the patient was on a cyclosporin taper at the time
of improvement. In addition, weakness was not agreed upon by the Agency as a neurologic
deficit to assess for TMA improvement.

73
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
(b) (6)

Approximately one day after the diagnosis of HSCT-TMA the patient developed PRES.
Cyclosporin had been discontinued the day prior to the PRES diagnosis. The patient improved
with blood pressure control. The patient developed euphoria, delirium, and agitation
approximately 4 days after starting narsoplimab-wuug and was managed with steroid taper
(previously had euphoria while receiving steroids) and supportive treatment. Approximately 25
days after starting narsoplimab-wuug the patient developed delirium and confusion in the setting
of uncontrolled hyperglycemia and self-discontinuation of lorazepam and haloperidol. His
neurologic symptoms improved.
It is difficult to determine if the patient’s neurologic improvement was related to narsoplimabwuug. While PRES can be a manifestation of HSCT-TMA, it appears the patient improved with
blood pressure control, the timing of the improvement in PRES is unclear in the patient narrative
and therefore the impact of narsoplimab-wuug is unclear. Euphoria, delirium and confusion
occurred after narsoplimab-wuug was started so it is therefore confounded by a new treatment
and was managed with multiple other therapies including hyperglycemia control, steroid taper,
lorazepam and haloperidol. It is the reviewer’s opinion that this patient should be considered a
non-responder.
(b) (6)

The patient experienced intermittent seizures starting 5 days prior to narsoplimab-wuug therapy.
The patient was treated with antiepileptics and narsoplimab-wuug.
Seizures can be a manifestation of HSCT-TMA. It is challenging to determine if seizure
improvement was due to narsoplimab-wuug or treatment with antiepileptics. Of note, the patient
was subsequently found to have post-transplant lymphoproliferative disorder with a cerebellar
lesion. This could have also contributed to the seizures.

Time to Response and Duration of Response
Time to Response
Response was assessed anytime postbaseline therefore, there was a wide range of response times.
As stated in the Applicant’s protocol for Study TMA-001, the primary endpoint which was based
on HSCT-TMA laboratory markers could be assessed anytime post baseline. There is a wide
range of response times from 7 to 228 days, with a median response of 36 days. There were two
patients who met response criteria >100 days after starting narsoplimab-wuug based on HSCT(b) (6)
(b) (6)
TMA laboratory markers, patient
at 228 days and patient
at 153 days. Patient
narratives can be found in Section 16. The remainder of patients had a response between days 777. It seems unlikely that these two outlier responses can confidently be attributed to
(b) (6)
narsoplimab-wuug. It is therefore, in the clinical reviewer’s opinion that patients
and
(b) (6)
should not be called responders. (Refer to Table 28 for brief narratives for these
patients).
In the TMA-001 study protocol, time to response was defined as the time from the first dose of
narsoplimab-wuug to the first date of platelet count and LDH response achieved. Of note,
although per the protocol laboratory assessments were to be performed weekly, many patients
who were hospitalized had more frequent laboratory tests. Time to response, based on these
TMA markers was 36 days (range: 7-228 days). As this is not a complete definition of the
primary endpoint the Applicant was asked to submit a time to response analysis based on all
three components of the primary endpoint.
In the IR response from the Applicant, the Applicant presented time to response based on all
three components of the primary endpoint. Time to response was calculated as the number of
days between the date of the first dose of narsoplimab-wuug to the first date that the TMA
marker improvement and clinical benefit was achieved. Based on the Applicant’s analysis, the
median time to response was 57 days (range: 13 to 228 days). This consideration will be
important for providers caring for patients with HSCT-TMA. It remains difficult to determine

75
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
when providers should expect to see a response to narsoplimab-wuug given the wide range of
response times.
Duration of Response
Duration of response was defined as the time between the first date a response is achieved and
the first date the platelet count decreases by more than 50% from the maximum and LDH
increases to >150% of the upper limit of normal. The median duration of response was 561 days
(range: 5 to 823+ days). In total 6 patients experienced a relapse based on the duration of
response definition. As this definition of response is not a complete definition of the primary
endpoint the Applicant was asked to submit duration of response analysis based on all three
components of the primary endpoint.
In the IR response from the Applicant, the Applicant presented duration of response based on all
components of the primary endpoint. The Applicant defined relapse as having a platelet count
below a level equal to at least a 50% decrease from the maximum platelet count, an LDH level
greater than 1.5 times the upper limit of normal, and a creatinine increase ≥20% from the
minimum creatinine if the minimum creatinine is < ULN or an increase ≥40% from the
minimum creatinine if the minimum creatinine is ≥ ULN. No patient experienced worsening
organ function and transfusion worsening could not be defined for patients who achieved
freedom of transfusion given its definition. Based on the revised analysis the Applicant stated the
median duration of response could not be calculated. The range of duration of response was 13 to
794 days. Based on the revised analysis, 3 patients relapsed.
Summary
Taking into consideration the issues presented above and after review of each of the patient
(b) (6)
narratives, the clinical team identified five additional patients (Subject ID
(b) (6)
as not achieving response as defined in the protocol. The reasons
for excluding these patients as responders is that these 5 subjects did not meet at least 3
components of the response criteria. In a clinical setting, some of these patients could be
described as having a partial response but in terms of protocol defined response are considered
nonresponders. Based upon this analysis, the proportion of patients in Study TMA-001 who
responded to narsoplimab-wuug would be 12/28; 42.9% [CI: 25%, 63]of patients. A detailed
summary of the patient’s narratives can be found in Section 16; in addition, Table 25 provides a
brief synopsis of the reviewers’ concerns.

6.3.2. External Literature-Based Validation of NonTreated Response Rate.
Issue
The Applicant provided an external literature review to justify the response rate in untreated
patients.
79
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
Background
The Applicant submitted an external literature review during the review cycle of the application.
The submission of this additional data warranted and required additional time for analysis and
thus a major amendment was issued to the Applicant on May 19, 2021.
The study design was a retrospective patient level data from published publications between
2000-2020 identified using PubMed electronic database search. The criteria for publication
selection were adult patients and any component of the primary response endpoint used in Study
TMA-001 trial and report of response and at least 1 risk factor present in the Study TMA-001
trial patients. The literature search identified 459 manuscripts of which 35 met the predefined
criteria and 179 patients identified for the final analysis of which 149 are nonpharmacologically
treated patients.
Assessment
A summary of response and 100-day survival are provided in Table 27. Please note that in Study
OMS721-TMA-001, 100-day survival is defined as survival 100 days after diagnosis of HSCTTMA. The definition of 100-day survival was not always provided for the literature-based
review.

Assessment of survival (100-day survival) cannot be adequately assessed or compared due to
lack of uniform definition. In the transplantation setting, 100 day survival often refers to survival
100 days after Day 0 of the hematopoietic stem cell transplantation.
Conclusion
In Study TMA-001, the Applicant used a 15% efficacy threshold and this was based on expert
opinion only provided by the Applicant There are significant limitations to literature-based
review to support and verify this estimate. The Division sought expertise from Real World
Evidence (RWE) internal FDA expert regarding quality of data. The limitations include
unknown validity and accuracy of data, potential measurement errors associated with endpoints
(response rates) and definition of 100-day survival across different publications, propensity score
analysis performed on limited confounding variables, timing of treatment visits not standardized
across different publications, study conducted using convenience samples and likely selection
bias affects generalizability of the results. Therefore, the literature based review cannot be
considered sufficient to Estimate response rate in an untreated group.

6.3.3. Subject Matter Expert Analysis
Issue
The Applicant submitted a position paper by subject matter experts stating that the responses
observed in Study OMS721-001 represent a true response to treatment with narsoplimab and that
all 17 patients who Applicant identified are true responders.
The Division consulted with two subject matter experts to provide expert opinion.
Background
The Division sought input regarding the use of narsoplimab-wuug in patients with HSCT-TMA
and requested input to address the following questions:
•

Is there evidence of effectiveness for narsoplimab-wuug in the HSCT-TMA
population taking into account the rarity of the disease, no FDA available therapies,
trial limitations (single-arm trial), confounders in the interpretation of treatment effect
of the efficacy endpoint (concomitant medications and illnesses), and discrepancies
and inconsistencies in the application of the response definition for the subjects?

•

Is the response demonstrated in Study TMA-001 a significant change from what
would be expected based upon the natural history and accepted standard of care
treatment of this disease?

The Division provided the 28 narrative summaries, the clinical study report and background
document to the SGEs. The SGEs provided summary response documents and the Division also
met via teleconference with the SGEs to discuss responses.
Assessment
Response to the First Question
SGE number one reviewed all 28 cases and stated that 15/28 (%) patients appear to have a
response to treatment and that all patients enrolled in the study were consistent with patients who
had diagnosis of TMA.
SGE number two reviewed all 28 cases and stated that 16/28 (%) patients appeared to have a
response to treatment and that all patients enrolled in the study were consistent with patients who
had a diagnosis of TMA. Both SGE’s did note that response was not always durable and that
response consisted mostly of modest improvements in laboratory parameters of LDH or
haptoglobin. Both consultants did comment that they would likely the this drug in the treatment
of HSCT-TMA after failure of standard of conventional therapy (immunosuppressants
modifications, off-label use of C5 inhibitors).:

81
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
Response to the Second Question
SGE 2 did comment that he felt that the survival observed in the study was a deparature from
what would be expected for this severely affected population. SGE 1 also commented that the
response observed was an improvement from the known literature of response rates for patients
with HSCT-TMA with the caveat that there has been difficulties in uniform consensus on
diagnosis and response criteria. SGE 2 did note that the enrolled population appeared consistent
with a severe population and that survival observed was better than what one would expect from
similar population.

Conclusion
Overall, the SGE’s assessment of the individual narratives concluded that there was a response
rate of 50% to 54%. Both SGEs concluded that the patients enrolled in the study are
representative of a patient population with HSCT-TMA some who had severe HSCT-TMA. SGE
2 believed that the survival observed in the study was better than what one would expect from a
similar population.
Both SGEs noted that the limitation of the study is the lack of a control arm but based on clinical
experience, some patients had responses although not always robust responses. They also noted
that most of the responses were primarily based on LDH and haptoglobin. Both SGEs stated they
would likely use this medication in the clinical setting for patients with HSCT-TMA.
The SGE’s used clinical judgment to assess response rate but did not necessarily adhere to the
protocol definition of response rate. The opinions provided by the SGE’s did not alter the
conclusions of the clinical reviewers’ assessment of response rate.

Narsoplimab-wuug

6.3.4. Pediatric Indication
Issue
The Applicant proposed to include a pediatric indication in the USPI but, there were no formal
pediatric studies.
Background
The Applicant proposed to include a pediatric indication including ages 2 years of age and older.
There are no approved therapies for HSCT-TMA in pediatric patients. The Applicant states that
MASP-2 levels increase by age 2 and remain stable throughout childhood and are similar to adult
levels. As stated in the Applicant’s clinical overview the rationale for the proposal of a pediatric
indication was based on dosing reasons of biological plausibility and consistency of the disease
mechanism, role and levels of MASP-2 and lectin pathway activation across broad age groups,
and the satisfactory therapeutic window enabled by weight-based dosing.
Assessment
No pediatric patients were treated in narsoplimab-wuug clinical trials. There was one 14-year-old
adolescent patient treated on compassionate use that appeared to respond (based on clinical
judgment) to narsoplimab-wuug. In the 90-day safety update it was reported four additional
patients were treated with narsoplimab-wuug. Given the very limited experience of pediatric
patients with narsoplimab-wuug the clinical review team does not agree with including a
pediatric indication. It is not clear if pediatric patients will respond the same to narsoplimabwuug as adults, in addition pediatric patients may have different comorbidities that may impact
response. In addition, pediatric patients are more often transplanted for nonmalignant conditions
compared to adults, and it’s not clear how the indication for transplant may impact response to
narsoplimab-wuug. Lastly, there is not sufficient safety data in the pediatric population.
Conclusion
The clinical review team believes that the sponsor must perform a pediatric assessment in order
to support a pediatric indication and that full extrapolation from adults is unlikely to be
acceptable.

7. Risk and Risk Management
7.1. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on
Nonclinical Data
Preclinical safety of OMS721 was evaluated in a standard battery of nonclinical studies
including safety pharmacology studies, repeat-dose toxicity studies in mice and monkeys for
durations up to 4 and 26 weeks, respectively, reproductive toxicity studies in mice and rabbits, a
juvenile toxicity study in mice, and a local tolerance study in rabbits. There were no nonclinical

83
Integrated Review Template, version 2.0 (04/23/2020)
Reference ID: 4873032

BLA 761152
Narsoplimab-wuug
safety issues of concern that substantially impacted clinical development as assessed by these
studies.
Note that functional potency of OMS721, measured as inhibition of C4 and C3 activation in
serum, is lower in nonclinical species than in humans; however, exposures evaluated in the
pivotal toxicology studies achieved near- to complete inhibition of lectin-mediated complement
activation, enabling a reasonable toxicological assessment of inhibiting the intended
pharmacological target, MASP-2, by OMS721.
Cardiovascular and respiratory safety pharmacology assessments in monkeys and a CNS safety
pharmacology assessment in mice showed no OMS721-related effects. In repeat-dose toxicology
studies in mice and monkeys, minimal to mild subcutaneous injection site reactions were
observed in both species. Sporadic increases were observed in AST and ALT in mice and
monkeys in 28-day studies but were not present in the 26-week studies. Intravenous
administration was also assessed, resulting in one instance of mild edema and minimal
adventitial necrosis at the injection site, and one female that experienced pronounced decreases
in total protein and albumin during treatment which became more severe during the recovery
period. The adverse response of this female is of limited relevance to clinical risk, as the
proposed clinical route of administration is subcutaneous, not intravenous. The no observed
adverse effect level (NOAEL) identified in the 26-week monkey study was 300 mg/kg/week by
the SC route (38-fold the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD)), while the NOAEL for
the IV route was not established due to irreversible changes in protein and albumin levels at
150 mg/kg, the only IV dose studied (43-fold the MRHD).

7.3. Potential Safety Concerns Identified Through
Postmarket Experience
Narsoplimab-wuug is not approved or marketed in the United States or worldwide.

7.4. FDA Approach to the Safety Review
The Applicant’s safety database consisted of 6 completed or ongoing studies and a
compassionate use program. The safety population consisted of all patients or subjects who
received any amount of study drug.
For this review, the clinical review team considered the primary safety population to consist of
the 28 patients with HSCT-TMA from Study OMS721-TMA-001 (TMA-001). The supportive
safety population consisted of 256 patients from studies OMS721-NHV-001, OMS721-NHV002, TMA-001 (excluding the 28 patients with HSCT-TMA included in the primary safety
population), OMS-721-GNP-001, OMS721-IGA-001, OMS721-HUS-002, and the
compassionate use program. Study OMS721-IGA-001 is ongoing and blinded and is minimally
discussed in the safety review.
The data cutoff date for the safety assessments submitted in the initial BLA was December 20,
2019. The 90-Day safety update provided additional safety data collected from the 2 ongoing
clinical trials from December 21, 2019 to August 31, 2020 (Studies OMS721 HUS-002 and
OMS721-IGA-001) and safety data from compassionate use patients from December 21, 2019 to
October 31, 2020.
Review of safety was based upon:
•

CSR for studies

•

Protocol for studies

•

Data sets for the populations described above

•

Summary of clinical safety

•

90-day safety update

•

Patient narratives

•

Case report forms

Case report forms and narratives were provided and reviewed for adverse events (AEs) of
interest, serious adverse events (SAEs), and deaths that occurred in the primary and supportive
safety populations. Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) of special interest included
infections.
No major issues were identified with respect to recording, coding, and categorizing AEs. The
analysis dataset for adverse events (ADAE.xpt) data file was reviewed for accuracy of translation
of the verbatim to preferred term by manual review. Based on this review a modified table
(Table 30) was produced where additions of preferred terms were added based on the verbatim

preferred terms were grouped by standardized groupings of related preferred terms through FDA
Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities queries (FMQs). Additional analyses were
provided by the Clinical Data Scientist support team. The clinical review team did not identify
any major data quality or integrity issues that precluded performing a safety review.

7.5. Adequacy of Clinical Safety Database
The clinical safety database is limited in patients with HSCT-TMA who were exposed but
adequate given the rarity of HSCT-TMA and the availability of data from non-HSCT-TMA
patients exposed to study drug to provide supportive safety data. In Study TMA-001, 28 patients
with HSCT-TMA received at least one dose of narsoplimab-wuug. In total, 24 (85.7%) patients
received weight-based dosing at 4 mg/kg IV, after protocol amendment 11.1, 4 (14.3%) patients
received fixed doses of 370 mg IV. The mean duration of exposure was 6.7 weeks. A summary
of exposure is shown in Table 31.
Duration of exposure in the supportive safety population can be found in Table 123.
